) has already been investigated in several works, see, for instance,
[
28
,
29
,
20
]
. In the case of non-degenerate mobilities and vanishing vector fields, i.e.,
\mathbf{v}\equiv 0
and
\mathbf{w}\equiv 0
, the recent work
[
39
]
establishes the well-posedness of weak solutions together with the propagation of regularity. This section aims to extend these results to the case of non-trivial vector fields.

We start with the following result regarding the existence of global-in-time weak solutions.

Theorem A.1
.

Suppose that the mobility functions
m_{\Omega},m_{\Gamma}\in C([-1,1])
satisfy
2.16
and that the potentials satisfy
(A5)
-(
(A6)
). Let
K\in(0,\infty)
,
L\in[0,\infty]
,
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}})
, and let
(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
be an arbitrary initial datum satisfying

\displaystyle\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\|\psi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\leq 1.

(A.2a)

In addition, we assume that

\displaystyle\beta\,\textnormal{mean}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0}),\ \textnormal{mean}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\in(-1,1),\hskip 10.00002pt\text{if~ }L\in[0,\infty),

(A.2b)

and

\displaystyle{\langle\phi_{0}\rangle}_{\Omega},\ {\langle\psi_{0}\rangle}_{\Gamma}\in(-1,1),\hskip 10.00002pt\text{if~ }L=\infty.

(A.2c)

Then, there exists a global-in-time weak solution
(\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
to (
A.1
) such that:

(i)

The weak solution satisfies

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle\in BC_{w}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1})\cap L^{4}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{2})\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{W}^{2,p}),

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(\mu,\theta)

\displaystyle\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}),

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))

\displaystyle\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{L}^{p})

for any
2\leq p<\infty
, and it holds

\displaystyle|\phi|<1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.~in~}Q\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt|\psi|<1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.~in~}\Sigma.

(A.3)

(ii)

The weak solution solves (
A.1
) in the following variational sense

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\langle(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi),(\zeta,\xi)\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}}+\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\quad=-\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi)\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi)\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma-\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta-\mu)(\beta\xi-\zeta)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\end{split}

(A.4)

a.e. on
(0,\infty)
for all
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}
, where
(\mu,\theta)
are given by

\displaystyle\mu=-\Delta\phi+F^{\prime}(\phi)

\displaystyle\text{a.e.~in }Q,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\theta=-\Delta_{\Gamma}\psi+G^{\prime}(\psi)+\alpha\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi

\displaystyle\text{a.e.~on }\Sigma,

(A.6)

\displaystyle K\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi=\alpha\psi-\phi

\displaystyle\text{a.e.~on }\Sigma.

(A.7)

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle\phi|_{t=0}=\phi_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.~in~}\Omega\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\psi|_{t=0}=\psi_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.~on~}\Gamma.

(iii)

The functions
\phi
and
\psi
satisfy the mass conservation law

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\beta\int_{\Omega}\phi(t)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi(t)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=\beta\int_{\Omega}\phi_{0}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{0}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,&\textnormal{if }L\in[0,\infty),\\
\int_{\Omega}\phi(t)\;\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\phi_{0}\;\mathrm{d}x\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\int_{\Gamma}\psi(t)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{0}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,&\textnormal{if }L=\infty\end{cases}

(A.8)

for all
t\in[0,\infty)
.

(iv)

The weak solution satisfies the energy inequality

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi(t),\psi(t))+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi)|\nabla\mu|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi)|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\chi(L)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta-\mu)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

(A.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mu\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

for a.e.
t\in[0,\infty)
including
t=0
.

(v)

The following estimates hold

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{1})}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime})}^{2}+\|(\nabla\mu,\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta)\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\\
&\qquad+\chi(L)\|\beta\theta-\mu\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),\end{split}

(A.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{W}^{2,p})}^{2}+\|(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{L}^{p})}^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq C_{p}\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),\hskip-20.00003pt\end{split}

(A.11)

\displaystyle\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L^{4}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{2})}^{4}\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big)^{2}

(A.12)

for any
2\leq p<\infty
. Here, the constants
C
and
C_{p}
are independent of the initial conditions
(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
and the velocity fields
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
.

Proof.

Let
T>0
. It was already established in
[
20
, Theorem 3.2 and Theorem 3.3]
that there exists a weak solution
(\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
to (
A.1
) on a finite time interval
(0,T)
for
T>0
such that

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle\in BC_{w}([0,T);\mathcal{H}^{1})\cap L^{4}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{2})\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{W}^{2,p}),

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(\mu,\theta)

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}),

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{p})

for any
2\leq p<\infty
, and the weak solution satisfies the condition
(ii)
,
(iii)
and
(iv)
. To establish the estimates in
(v)
, we make use of the energy inequality (
(iv)
). First, using (
A.3
), we have by Young’s inequality

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mu\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{\min\{1,m_{\ast}\}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\mu|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\min\{1,m_{\ast}\}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{1}{2\min\{1,m_{\ast}\}}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s.

Recalling (
(iv)
), it readily follows that

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi(t),\psi(t))+\frac{\min\{1,m_{\ast}\}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+\frac{1}{2\min\{1,m_{\ast}\}}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s

for a.e.
t\in(0,\infty)
. Then, since by assumption
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
and
E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
is finite, we can use again (
A.3
) to find that

\displaystyle\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{1})}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big)

(A.13)

as well as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big).

(A.14)

Next, a comparison argument in (
A.4
) yields the estimate

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}\leq C\big(\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}+\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}\big),

(A.15)

from which we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big).

(A.16)

Then, as shown in
[
29
]
, we have the estimate

\displaystyle|\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta)|\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}\big),

(A.17)

see also (
4.26
), which implies together with (
A.14
) that
(\mu,\theta)\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1})
. Now, noticing that

\displaystyle-\Delta\phi+F_{0}^{\prime}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\mu^{\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~in~}\Omega,

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\Gamma}\psi+G_{0}^{\prime}(\psi)+\alpha\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi

\displaystyle=\theta^{\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~on~}\Gamma,

\displaystyle K\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi

\displaystyle=\alpha\psi-\phi

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~on~}\Gamma,

almost everywhere on
(0,\infty)
, where
(\mu^{\ast},\theta^{\ast})=(\mu-c_{F}\phi,\theta-c_{G}\psi)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
, an application of
[
20
, Proposition 6.5]
together with (
A.13
) and (
A.17
) yields that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,p}}+\|(F_{0}^{\prime}(\phi),G_{0}^{\prime}(\psi))\|_{\mathcal{L}^{p}}&\leq C_{p}\big(1+\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\big)\\
&\leq C_{p}(1+\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}\big)\end{split}

(A.18)

almost everywhere on
(0,\infty)
for all
2\leq p<\infty
. The latter entails that
(\phi,\psi)\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{W}^{2,p})
as well as
(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{L}^{p})
together with the estimate (
A.11
). Lastly, recalling that
K\in(0,\infty)
, we apply
[
20
, Corollary 5.4]
and find that

\displaystyle\|(-\Delta\phi,-\Delta_{\Gamma}\psi+\alpha\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu-c_{F}\phi,\theta-c_{G}\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\big)\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\big).

Thus, by elliptic regularity theory for systems with bulk-surface coupling (see, e.g.,
[
27
, Theorem 3.3]
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),

which provides
(\phi,\psi)\in L^{4}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{2})
.
∎

Our next result is concerned with the uniqueness of weak solutions to (
A.1
). In the non-convective case, this was first shown for the Cahn–Hilliard equation with dynamic boundary conditions in
[
39
]
.

Theorem A.2
.

Suppose that the mobility functions satisfy
(A4)
and the potentials
(A5)
. Let
K\in(0,\infty)
,
L\in[0,\infty]
, and let
(\phi_{0}^{1},\psi_{0}^{1}),(\phi_{0}^{2},\psi_{0}^{2})\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
be two pairs of initial data satisfying (
A.2
) as well as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\textnormal{mean}(\phi_{0}^{1},\psi_{0}^{1})=\textnormal{mean}(\phi_{0}^{2},\psi_{0}^{2}),&\textnormal{if }L\in[0,\infty),\\
{\langle\phi_{0}^{1}\rangle}_{\Omega}={\langle\phi_{0}^{2}\rangle}_{\Omega}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad{\langle\psi_{0}^{1}\rangle}_{\Gamma}={\langle\psi_{0}^{2}\rangle}_{\Gamma},&\textnormal{if }L=\infty,\end{cases}

(A.19)

and let
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{1}),(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\tau}(\Gamma))
be prescribed velocity fields. Consider two weak solutions
(\phi_{1},\psi_{1},\mu_{1},\theta_{1})
and
(\phi_{2},\psi_{2},\mu_{2},\theta_{2})
originating from
(\phi_{0}^{1},\psi_{0}^{1})
and
(\phi_{0}^{2},\psi_{0}^{2})
with velocity fields
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{1})
and
(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})
, respectively. Then, for any
T>0
, there exists a positive constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(\phi_{1}(t)-\phi_{2}(t),\psi_{1}(t)-\psi_{2}(t))\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq\|(\phi_{0}^{1}-\phi_{0}^{2},\psi_{0}^{1}-\psi_{0}^{2})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}P(\tau)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}_{1}(s)-\mathbf{v}_{2}(s),\mathbf{w}_{1}(s)-\mathbf{w}_{2}(s))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}P(\tau)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\;\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(A.20)

for almost all
t\in[0,T]
and any
\omega>0
, where

\displaystyle P=C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{2},\theta_{2})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{1},\partial_{t}\psi_{1})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big),

and the constant
C>0
depends only on
\Omega
, the initial data and the parameters of the system.

Proof.

Let
(\phi_{0}^{1},\psi_{0}^{1})
and
(\phi_{0}^{2},\psi_{0}^{2})
be two pairs of admissible initial data satisfying (
A.2
) as well as (
A.19
). Additionally, let
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{1}),(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\tau}(\Gamma))
. Consider the corresponding weak solutions
(\phi_{1},\psi_{1},\mu_{1},\theta_{1})
and
(\phi_{2},\psi_{2},\mu_{2},\theta_{2})
.
Then, defining
(\phi,\psi)=(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2},\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big<(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi),(\zeta,\xi)\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}}\\
&\quad=\int_{\Omega}\big(\phi_{1}\mathbf{v}+\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(\psi_{1}\mathbf{w}+\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\qquad-\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi_{1})\nabla(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{1})\nabla_{\Gamma}(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\qquad-\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}\big(\beta(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\big)(\beta\xi-\zeta)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\qquad-\int_{\Omega}\big(m_{\Omega}(\phi_{1})-m_{\Omega}(\phi_{2})\big)\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\big(m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{1})-m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{2})\big)\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{2}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\end{split}

(A.21)

a.e. on
(0,\infty)
for all
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}
, as well as

\displaystyle-\Delta\phi+F^{\prime}(\phi_{1})-F^{\prime}(\phi_{2})

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~on~}Q,

(A.22a)

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\Gamma}\psi+G^{\prime}(\psi_{1})-G^{\prime}(\psi_{2})+\alpha\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi

\displaystyle=\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~on~}\Sigma,

(A.22b)

\displaystyle K\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi

\displaystyle=\alpha\psi-\phi

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~on~}\Sigma.

(A.22c)

Now, we multiply (
A.22a
) with
\phi
and (
A.22b
) with
\psi
, integrate over
\Omega
and
\Gamma
, respectively, and perform integration by parts. Adding the resulting equations leads to

\displaystyle\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\big(F_{0}^{\prime}(\phi_{1})-F_{1}^{\prime}(\phi_{2})\big)\phi\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(G_{0}^{\prime}(\psi_{1})-G_{1}^{\prime}(\psi_{2})\big)\psi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}\big(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\phi\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\big(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\big)\psi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(A.23)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=c_{F}\int_{\Omega}\phi^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+c_{G}\int_{\Gamma}\psi^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

almost everywhere on
(0,\infty)
. Next, we want to rewrite the integrals involving the chemical potentials in terms of the solution operator
\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi_{1},\psi_{1}](\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)
. To this end, to make the following computations more readable, we use the abbreviation
\mathcal{S}_{1}(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)=\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi_{1},\psi_{1}](\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)
and find

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\int_{\Omega}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\phi\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})\psi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\quad=\big((\phi,\psi),\mathcal{S}_{1}(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\big)_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}-\int_{\Omega}\big(m_{\Omega}(\phi_{1})-m_{\Omega}(\phi_{2})\big)\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad-\int_{\Gamma}(m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{1})-m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{1})\big)\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{2}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Omega}\big(\phi_{1}\mathbf{v}+\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad+\int_{\Gamma}\big(\psi_{1}\mathbf{w}+\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\end{split}

(A.24)

a.e. on
(0,\infty)
.
Plugging the identity (
A.24
) back into (
A
) yields

\displaystyle\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\big(F_{0}^{\prime}(\phi_{1})-F_{0}^{\prime}(\phi_{2})\big)\phi\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(G_{0}^{\prime}(\psi_{1})-G_{0}^{\prime}(\psi_{2})\big)\psi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{S}_{1}(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\phi\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\psi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}\big(m_{\Omega}(\phi_{1})-m_{\Omega}(\phi_{2})\big)\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}^{\Omega}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\big(m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{1})-m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{2})\big)\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{2}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}^{\Gamma}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\big(\phi_{1}\mathbf{v}+\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(\psi_{1}\mathbf{w}+\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=c_{F}\int_{\Omega}\phi^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+c_{G}\int_{\Gamma}\psi^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

Then, in view of the chain rule (cf.
[
39
, Eqn. (5.18)]
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\phi\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\psi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\quad=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta,[\phi_{1},\psi_{1}],\ast}^{2}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathcal{S}_{1}(\partial_{t}\phi_{1},\partial_{t}\psi_{1}),\big(m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{1})|\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)|^{2},m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{1})|\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)|^{2}\big)\Big)_{L,\beta},\end{split}

(A.25)

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta,[\phi_{1},\psi_{1}],\ast}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}\leq c_{F}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{G}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3},

(A.26)

where

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}(\partial_{t}\phi_{1},\partial_{t}\psi_{1}),(m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{1})|\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)|^{2},m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{1})|\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)|^{2})\big)_{L,\beta},

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\big(m_{\Omega}(\phi_{1})-m_{\Omega}(\phi_{2})\big)\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}^{\Omega}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{1})-m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{2})\big)\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{2}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}^{\Gamma}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

and

\displaystyle I_{3}=\int_{\Omega}\big(\phi_{1}\mathbf{v}+\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(\psi_{1}\mathbf{w}+\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

The terms
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
have already been estimated in
[
39
]
. Namely, it holds that

\displaystyle|I_{1}+I_{2}|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{8}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\big(\|(\mu_{2},\theta_{2})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{1},\partial_{t}\psi_{1})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{2},

see
[
39
, Eqns. (5.62)–(5.63)]
.
Furthermore, as (
2.12
) implies that

\displaystyle c_{F}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{G}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq C\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{8}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{2},

(A.27)

we only have to estimate the term
I_{3}
, which contains the convective contributions. To this end, we first observe that

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{\Omega}\big(\phi_{1}\mathbf{v}+\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\big(\psi_{1}\mathbf{w}+\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{\Omega}\nabla\phi_{1}\cdot\mathbf{v}\,\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{1}\cdot\mathbf{w}\,\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

(A.28)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\Big|\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|.

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
A
), we use Hölder’s, Sobolev’s and the bulk-surface Poincaré inequality and deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Omega}\nabla\phi_{1}\cdot\mathbf{v}\,\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{1}\cdot\mathbf{w}\,\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\phi_{1}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\frac{2(1+\omega)}{\omega}}(\Omega)}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L^{\frac{2(1+\omega)}{\omega}}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{1}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+C\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}

for any
\omega>0
.
For the second term on the right-hand side of (
A
), we use a similar argument to
[
18
, Lemma 5.1]
, see also
[
16
]
. However, since in our case we have non-degenerate mobility functions, additional terms are coming from (
2.14
) that have to be handled suitably. To be precise, exploiting (
2.1
), (
2.3
), (
2.12
) and (
2.14
), we find that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}_{2}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}_{2}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{4}}\|(\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi),\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi),\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Omega}(\phi,\psi),\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}(\phi,\psi))\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times\Big(\|(\nabla\phi_{1},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{1})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\Big(\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla\phi_{1},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{1})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\Big(\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\|\mathcal{S}_{1}(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}.

Consequently, we find the differential inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta,[\phi_{1},\psi_{1}],\ast}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+P(t)\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{L,\beta,[\phi_{1},\psi_{1}],\ast}^{2},

(A.29)

where

\displaystyle P(\cdot)=C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{2},\theta_{2})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{1},\partial_{t}\psi_{1})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\in L^{1}(0,T).

An application of Gronwall’s lemma readily yields the desired conclusion (
A.20
), and thus the uniqueness of weak solutions to (
A.1
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Our next result is concerned with the existence of strong solutions to (
A.1
).

Theorem A.3
.

We suppose that the mobility functions satisfy
(A4)
and that the potentials satisfy
(A5)
. Let
K\in(0,\infty)
and
L\in[0,\infty]
, and let the initial condition
(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
satisfy (
A.2
), as well as the following compatibility condition:

(C)

There exists
(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta}
such that for all
(\eta,\vartheta)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
it holds

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\Omega}\mu_{0}\eta\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{0}\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\phi_{0}\cdot\nabla\eta+F^{\prime}(\phi_{0})\eta\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{0}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\vartheta+G^{\prime}(\psi_{0})\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\quad+\chi(K)\int_{\Gamma}(\alpha\psi_{0}-\phi_{0})(\alpha\vartheta-\eta)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.\end{aligned}

For the velocity fields
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
, we assume one of the following conditions:

(i)

(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}(0,\infty;\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})\cap L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}})
for some
\omega>0
,

(ii)

(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})\cap L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}})
.

Then, the corresponding unique weak solution
(\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
to (
A.1
) satisfies

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{W}^{2,p}),\hskip 10.00002pt(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime})\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1}),

\displaystyle(\mu,\theta)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{3})\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi)),\ (F^{\prime\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime\prime}(\psi))\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{L}^{p})

for any
2\leq p<\infty
. Moreover, the equations (
A.1a
)-(
A.1b
) are satisfied almost everywhere on
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, while (
A.1c
)-(
A.1d
) and the boundary conditions (
A.1e
)-(
A.1f
) are satisfied almost everywhere on
\Gamma\times(0,\infty)
.

Remark A.4
.

Concerning the assumptions on the mobility functions and the velocity fields in Theorem
A.3
, we remark the following. For
m_{\Omega},m_{\Gamma}\in C^{1}([-1,1])
and Leray-velocity fields, i.e.,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})\cap L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}),

one can also show the existence of a strong solution to (
A.1
). This can be done by combining the approaches of
[
20
, Theorem 3.6]
and
[
39
, Theorem 3.4]
(note also
[
20
, Remark 3.7]
). However, as the mobility functions only lie in
C^{1}([-1,1])
, Theorem
A.2
about the uniqueness is not applicable. Nonetheless, if
L\in(0,\infty]
, the uniqueness of strong solutions to (
A.1
) can be achieved. The case
L=0
is only admissible if we additionally take
K=0
, provided that
\alpha\neq 0,\beta\neq 0
, and the following compatibility condition on the potentials:

\displaystyle F(\alpha s)=\alpha\beta G(s)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}s\in[-1,1].

In this case, we have
(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta}
, entailing that it is an admissible test function in (
A.4
). We do not present the proof of this uniqueness result at this point, as the computations are the same as in the proof of Theorem
3.4
, see, in particular, (
5
) and the corresponding estimates (
5.10
)-(
5.18
).

Proof.

Case
(i)
: In this case, we intend to apply the continuous dependence estimate (
A.29
).
Namely, for any
h\in(0,1)
, we apply (
A.29
) to
(\phi_{1},\psi_{1})=(\phi(\cdot+h),\psi(\cdot+h))
and
(\phi_{2},\psi_{2})=(\phi,\psi)
with given vector fields
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{1})=(\mathbf{v}(\cdot+h),\mathbf{w}(\cdot+h))
and
(\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{w}_{2})=(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
. After dividing the resulting inequality by
h^{2}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(t),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(t))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi(t+h),\psi(t+h)],\ast}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(t),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(t))\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{1}{2}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{v}(t),\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{w}(t))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+P_{h}(t)\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(t),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(t))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi(t+h),\psi(t+h)],\ast}^{2},\end{split}

(A.30)

where

\displaystyle P_{h}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi(\cdot+h),\partial_{t}\psi(\cdot+h))\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|(\phi(\cdot+h),\psi(\cdot+h))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\in L^{1}(0,T)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for any~}T>0.

Before applying the Gronwall lemma to (
A.30
), we aim to control the initial data. To this end, we can argue similarly as for (
A.26
). Now, instead, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}+\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq c_{F}\|\phi-\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{G}\|\psi-\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\big((\mu_{0},\theta_{0}),\mathcal{S}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\big)_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi]}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Omega}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Gamma}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(A.31)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt\big(m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi)|\nabla\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Omega}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})|^{2},m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi)|\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Gamma}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})|^{2}\big)\Big)_{L,\beta}.

The first two terms on the right-hand side of (
A
) can be estimated analogously to (
A.27
), namely,

\displaystyle c_{F}\|\phi-\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{G}\|\psi-\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta[\phi,\psi],\ast}^{2},

while the last term has already been estimated in
[
39
, Eqn. (5.62)]
as

\displaystyle\bigg|-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt\big(m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi)|\nabla\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Omega}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})|^{2},m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi)|\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Gamma}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})|^{2}\big)\Big)_{L,\beta}\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{4}\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\Big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\Big)\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}^{2}.

Then, for the third term on the right-hand side of (
A
) we simply use Hölder’s inequality and the boundedness of the mobility functions to find that

\displaystyle\big((\mu_{0},\theta_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\big)_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi]}\leq C\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast},

whereas for the convective terms, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Omega}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}^{\Gamma}[\phi,\psi](\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}

in view of (
A.3
). Collecting these estimates, we have the following differential inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta[\phi,\psi],\ast}^{2}

(A.32)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\Big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}+\big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}.

An application of a quadratic variant of the Gronwall lemma (see, e.g.,
[
7
, Lemma A.5]
) yields

\displaystyle\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}\big)t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\|(\phi-\phi_{0},\psi-\psi_{0})\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}\;\mathrm{d}s.

Then, we take
t=h
and divide the inequality by
h
to obtain

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(0),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(0))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\int_{0}^{h}\big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(0),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(0))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi,\psi],\ast}\;\mathrm{d}s.

Now, we can apply the integrated version of the Gronwall lemma to this inequality and find that

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(0),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(0))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi(\cdot+h),\psi(\cdot+h)],\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}\big)

(A.33)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times\Bigg(1+\int_{0}^{h}\big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\exp\Big(\int_{s}^{h}\big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\big)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\;\mathrm{d}s\Bigg).

Now that we have a suitable control of the initial data, we use again the Gronwall lemma for the differential inequality (
A.30
) and get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(t),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(t))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi(\cdot+h),\psi(\cdot+h)],\ast}^{2}&\leq\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(0),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(0))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi(\cdot+h),\psi(\cdot+h)],\ast}^{2}\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}P_{h}(\tau)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\\
&\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\exp\Big(C\int_{s}^{t}P_{h}(\tau)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\;\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(A.34)

Then, noting on

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{v}(t),\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{w}(t))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}(s),\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}(s))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}\;\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}(s),\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}(s))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}\;\mathrm{d}s=\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}(t),\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}(t))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}\end{split}

(A.35)

for a.e.
t\in(0,\infty)
, and (
A
), we observe that the right-hand side of (
A.34
) is uniformly bounded in
h\in(0,1)
. Moreover, by (
A.10
) and (
A.12
), for any
h\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}P_{h}(\tau)\;\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau.

(A.36)

Consequently, taking the limit
h\rightarrow 0
in (
A.34
), and noting on (
A
), (
A.35
) and (
A.36
) yields

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}\phi(t),\partial_{t}\psi(t))\|_{L,\beta,\ast}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\times\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big).

(A.37)

Thus, we find a constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in(0,1)}\|(\partial_{t}\phi(t),\partial_{t}\psi(t))\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}\leq C_{0}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for~a.e.~}t\in(0,1).

(A.38)

To derive a global control in time, we report the following uniform Gronwall lemma, which can be found, e.g., in
[
41
, Chapter III, Lemma 1.1]
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
f:[t_{0},\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be an absolutely continuous positive function, and
g,h
two positive locally summable functions on
[t_{0},\infty)
, which satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}f(t)

\displaystyle\leq g(t)f(t)+h(t)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for~a.e.~}t\geq t_{0},

and

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+r}g(s)\;\mathrm{d}s\leq a_{1},\hskip 10.00002pt\int_{t}^{t+r}h(s)\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq a_{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\int_{t}^{t+r}f(s)\;\mathrm{d}s\leq a_{3}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for all~}t\geq t_{0},

where
r,a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
are positive constants. Then it holds

\displaystyle f(t)\leq\left(\frac{a_{3}}{r}+a_{2}\right)e^{a_{1}}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}t\geq t_{0}+r.

Since

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}P_{h}(s)\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),\\
&\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\phi(s),\partial_{t}^{h}\psi(s))\|_{L,\beta,[\phi(\cdot+h),\psi(\cdot+h)],\ast}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),\\
&\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{v}(s),\partial_{t}^{h}\mathbf{w}(s))\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}(0,\infty;\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma))}^{2},\end{split}

(A.39)

an application of Lemma
A.5
with
t_{0}=0
and
r=1
shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(\partial_{t}\phi(t),\partial_{t}\psi(t))\|_{L,\beta,\ast}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}(0,\infty;\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma))}^{2}\big)\\
&\qquad\times\exp\Big(C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big)\Big)\qquad\text{for~all~}t\geq 1.\end{split}

(A.40)

Here, we have already taken the limit
h\rightarrow 0
and used again the norm equivalence on
\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{L,\beta}
(see (
2.11
)).
The latter implies together with (
A.38
) that
(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime})
, and the estimate (
A.40
) holds for a.e.
t\in(0,\infty)
. Next, we integrate (
A.30
) over the time interval
[t,t+1]
for
t\geq 0
, and utilize the estimates (
A.39
) and (
A.40
). By passing to the limit
h\rightarrow 0
in the corresponding estimate and using again the Poincaré inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\partial_{t}\phi(s),\partial_{t}\psi(s))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\Big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}(0,\infty;\mathbf{L}^{1+\omega}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{1}(\Gamma))}^{2}\Big)

(A.41)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times\bigg(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\Big)\exp\Big(C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big)\bigg),

which gives
(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1})
. Then, a comparison argument in (
A.4
) together with (
A.40
) and
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
further shows that

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in(0,\infty)}\|(\mu(t),\theta(t))\|_{L,\beta}\leq C\big(\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}\big).

(A.42)

Thus, in light of (
A.17
) and (
A.40
), we have
(\mu,\theta)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{1})
by exploiting the bulk-surface Poincaré inequality. By (
A.18
), we learn that

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{W}^{2,p}),\hskip 20.00003pt(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{L}^{p})

(A.43)

for any
2\leq p<\infty
. Moreover, by arguing as for
[
39
, Theorem 3.7]
, we find

\displaystyle(F^{\prime\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime\prime}(\psi))\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{L}^{p})

for any
2\leq p<\infty
(see also
[
33
,
32
]
), from which we deduce that

\displaystyle(\phi,\psi)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{3})

by elliptic regularity theory for systems with bulk-surface coupling (see, e.g.,
[
27
, Theorem 3.3]
).
Lastly, to derive higher-order estimates for the chemical potentials, we first notice that

\displaystyle(\mu-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta),\theta-\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta))=-\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\partial_{t}\phi+\nabla\phi\cdot\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\psi+\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w}).

(A.44)

Then, by (
2.9
), (
2.12
), (
2.14
), (
A.42
) and (
A.43
), we have

\displaystyle\|(\mu-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta),\theta-\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\|\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\partial_{t}\phi+\nabla\phi\cdot\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\psi+\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(\|(\nabla\phi,\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\|(\phi,\psi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\|\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\partial_{t}\phi+\nabla\phi\cdot\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\psi+\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w})\|_{L,\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|(\partial_{t}\phi+\nabla\phi\cdot\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\psi+\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{L,\beta}+\|\mathcal{S}_{L,\beta}[\phi,\psi](\nabla\phi\cdot\mathbf{v},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w})\|_{L,\beta}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|(\nabla\phi\cdot\mathbf{v},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}+\|(\nabla\phi,\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi)\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{\infty}}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\big(\|(\mu,\theta)\|_{L,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\|(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)\|_{K,\alpha}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

Integrating the previous estimate in time from
t
to
t+1
for
t\geq 0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\mu-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta),\theta-\textnormal{mean}(\mu,\theta))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C.

(A.45)

In light of (
A.17
), the latter entails that
(\mu,\theta)\in L^{4}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{2})
.

Case
(ii)
: Now, we assume that
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})\cap L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}})
. Using a standard mollification, we find a sequence
\{(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}\cap\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})&\qquad\text{strongly in~}L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}),\\
&\qquad\text{weakly-$\ast$ in~}L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),\end{split}

(A.46)

as
k\rightarrow\infty
. For more details, we refer to
[
5
, S. 4.13-4.15]
.
In particular, according to
[
5
, S. 4.13 (2)]
, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})}\leq\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})}

(A.47)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Now, owing to the first part of the proof, the unique global weak solution
(\phi_{k},\psi_{k},\mu_{k},\theta_{k})
to (
A.1
), which exists according to Theorem
A.1
and Theorem
A.2
, is a strong solution with the regularities

\displaystyle(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{3}),\hskip 10.00002pt(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime})\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1}),

\displaystyle(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{2}),

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}(\phi_{k})

\displaystyle,G^{\prime}(\psi_{k})),\ (F^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{k}),G^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{k}))\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\mathcal{L}^{p})

for any
2\leq p<\infty
and all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, in view of (
A.5
)-(
A.7
), one can deduce the existence of the weak time derivative
(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k})
in the sense that
(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k})\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime})
, and it holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\langle(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(\eta,\vartheta)\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}&=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\partial_{t}\phi_{k}\cdot\nabla\eta+F^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{k})\partial_{t}\phi_{k}\eta\;\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad+\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\psi_{k}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\vartheta+G^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{k})\partial_{t}\psi_{k}\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\quad+\chi(K)\int_{\Gamma}(\alpha\partial_{t}\psi_{k}-\partial_{t}\phi_{k})(\alpha\vartheta-\eta)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\end{split}

(A.48)

a.e. on
(0,\infty)
for all
(\eta,\vartheta)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
and all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, see
[
20
, Proof of Theorem 3.6, Step 5.1]
for more details. Now, following verbatim the proof of Theorem
A.1
, it follows from (
A.47
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(\phi_{k}(t),\psi_{k}(t))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),

(A.49)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})^{\prime}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big),

(A.50)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}^{2}\big)

(A.51)

as well as

\displaystyle\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,p}}+\|(F^{\prime}(\phi_{k}),G^{\prime}(\psi_{k}))\|_{\mathcal{L}^{p}}\leq C\big(1+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.~in~}(0,\infty)

(A.52)

for any
2\leq p<\infty
. To establish the higher regularity estimates, we aim to apply the chain rule
[
39
, Proposition A.1]
. To this end, we first have to show that
(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}(0,\infty;\mathcal{H}^{3})
. Recalling (
2.6
) and (
A.44
), we have

\displaystyle\|(\mu_{k}-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}),\theta_{k}-\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\mathbf{1}_{\{0\}}(L)\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times\Bigg(\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\partial_{t}\phi_{k}+\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{\partial_{t}\psi_{k}+\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\Big\|\bigg(\frac{m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{k})\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\nabla\mu_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{k})\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(1+\mathbf{1}_{\{0\}}(L)\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\big)\Bigg(\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\partial_{t}\phi_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{\partial_{t}\psi_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}

(A.53)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\Big\|\bigg(\frac{m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{k})\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\nabla\mu}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{k})\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\eqqcolon C\big(1+\mathbf{1}_{\{0\}}(L)\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}\big)\big(I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}).

Using standard computations, one readily shows that

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq C\big(1+\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,4}}\big)\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}

(A.54)

and

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,4}}\big)\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,4}}\|(\mu_{k}-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}),\theta_{k}-\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}},

(A.55)

see
[
39
, Eqn. (6.10) ff.]
for more details. For the remaining term, we find with (
A.47
) that

\displaystyle\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\mathbf{w}_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}+\Big\|\bigg(\frac{m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{k})(\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{k})\nabla\phi_{k}}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})^{2}},\frac{m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{k})(\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{k})\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})^{2}}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\nabla(\nabla\phi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{k})}{m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})},\frac{\nabla_{\Gamma}(\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{k})}{m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})}\bigg)\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}

(A.56)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(\|(\nabla\phi_{k},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{\infty}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}+\|(\nabla\phi_{k},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}+\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,4}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|(\nabla\phi_{k},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{\infty}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,4}}\big(1+\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}+\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,4}}\big).

In view of these bounds, the regularity of the approximate solution
(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})
and the approximate velocity fields
(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})
entails that
(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{3})
. We stress that this bound is not yet uniform in
k\in\mathbb{N}
. In view of this regularity, we are in a position to apply
[
39
, Proposition A.1]
which yields
(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\in C([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L,\beta})
, and the following chain rule

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{k}-\mu_{k})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\big<(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(-\textnormal{div}(m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})\nabla\mu_{k}),-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k})+\beta m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mu_{k})\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}

(A.57)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{k})\partial_{t}\phi_{k}|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{k})\partial_{t}\psi_{k}|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

holds a.e. on
(0,\infty)
. Taking (
A.1a
)-(
A.1c
) as well as (
A.48
) into account, the dual pairing on the right-hand side of (
A
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big<(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(-\textnormal{div}(m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})\nabla\mu_{k}),-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{k})+\beta m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mu_{k})\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad=-\big<(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(\partial_{t}\phi_{k}+\textnormal{div}(\phi_{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}),\partial_{t}\psi_{k}+\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k}\mathbf{w}_{k}))\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad=-\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}-\int_{\Omega}F^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{k})|\partial_{t}\phi_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}G^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{k})|\partial_{t}\psi_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\qquad-\big<(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(\textnormal{div}(\phi_{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}),\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k}\mathbf{w}_{k}))\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.\end{split}

(A.58)

Hence, exploiting the convexity of
F_{0}
and
G_{0}
(see (
2.17
)), it follows from (
A
) and (
A.58
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{k}-\mu_{k})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\int_{\Omega}c_{F}|\partial_{t}\phi_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}c_{G}|\partial_{t}\psi_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(A.59)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{k})\partial_{t}\phi_{k}|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{k})\partial_{t}\psi_{k}|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\big<(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(\textnormal{div}(\phi_{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}),\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k}\mathbf{w}_{k}))\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

For the first two terms on the right-hand side of (
A
), we have already seen in the first part of the proof that these can be estimated as

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{F}\|\partial_{t}\phi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+c_{G}\|\partial_{t}\psi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{6}\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\Big(\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

(A.60)

Then, for the next two terms, we use again the computations made in
[
39
, Eqns. (6.17)–(6.18)]
, which show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}^{\prime}(\phi_{k})\partial_{t}\phi_{k}|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}^{\prime}(\psi_{k})\partial_{t}\psi_{k}|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{6}\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\Big(\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\Big)\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2},\end{split}

(A.61)

while for the last term, it has been established in
[
20
, Eqn. (7.58)]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\big<(\partial_{t}\mu_{k},\partial_{t}\theta_{k}),(\textnormal{div}(\phi_{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}),\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k}\mathbf{w}_{k}))\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\big|\\
&\quad\leq\big|\big((\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k}),(\textnormal{div}(\phi_{k}\mathbf{v}_{k}),\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k}\mathbf{w}_{k}))\big)_{K,\alpha}\big|\\
&\qquad+\Big|\int_{\Omega}F^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{k})\partial_{t}\phi_{k}\textnormal{div}(\phi_{k}\mathbf{v}_{k})\;\mathrm{d}x\Big|+\Big|\int_{\Gamma}G^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{k})\partial_{t}\psi_{k}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k}\mathbf{w}_{k})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{6}\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}+C\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+C\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.62)

Thus, collecting the estimates (
A.60
), (
A.61
) and (
A.62
), we end up with the differential inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{k}-\mu_{k})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq C\big(\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\big)+C\Big(\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\Big)\\
&\qquad\times\Big(\int_{\Omega}m_{\Omega}(\phi_{k})|\nabla\mu_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}m_{\Gamma}(\psi_{k})|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{k}|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{k}-\mu_{k})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)\end{split}

(A.63)

a.e. on
(0,\infty)
. Applying Gronwall’s lemma yields

\displaystyle\|(\mu_{k}(t),\theta_{k}(t)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\mu_{k}(0),\theta_{k}(0))\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{k},\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\times\exp\Big(C\int_{s}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)\;\mathrm{d}s.

As
(\mu_{k}(0),\theta_{k}(0))=(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})
, we readily infer from (
A.47
), (
A.49
), (
A.50
), and (
A.51
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|(\mu_{k}(t),\theta_{k}(t)\|_{L,\beta}^{2}&\leq C\Big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
&\qquad\times\exp\Big(C\big(1+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big).\end{split}

(A.64)

To derive a global control in time, we aim to use again the uniform Gronwall lemma
A.5
. In view of (
A.47
), (
A.50
) and (
A.51
), we obtain similarly to the first part of the proof that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\geq 1}\|(\mu_{k}(t),\theta_{k}(t))\|_{L,\beta}^{2}&\leq C\Big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
&\qquad\times\exp\Big(C\big(1+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big).\end{split}

(A.65)

Next, integrating (
A.63
) in time from
t
to
t+1
for any
t\geq 0
, we readily deduce on account of (
A.64
), (
A.65
) and the bulk-surface Poincaré inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{k},\partial_{t}\psi_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s&\leq C\Big(1+\|(\mu_{0},\theta_{0})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
&\quad\times\Big(C\big(1+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big)\exp\Big(C\big(1+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big).\end{split}

(A.66)

Now, based on the estimates (
A.64
) and (
A.65
), it follows from (
A.52
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(\phi_{k}(t),\psi_{k}(t))\|_{\mathcal{W}^{2,p}}+\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(F^{\prime}(\phi_{k}(t)),G^{\prime}(\psi_{k}(t)))\|_{\mathcal{L}^{p}}\leq C_{p}

(A.67)

for any
2\leq p<\infty
. Moreover, by arguing as for
[
39
, Theorem 3.7]
(see also
[
33
,
32
]
), we find

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(F^{\prime\prime}(\phi_{k}(t)),G^{\prime\prime}(\psi_{k}(t))\|_{\mathcal{L}^{p}}+\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(\phi_{k}(t),\psi_{k}(t))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}\leq C_{p}^{\prime}.

Next, following the same computations as for (
A.45
), we find

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\mu_{k}-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}),\theta_{k}-\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C.

(A.68)

Then, on account of (
A.54
)-(
A
), we deduce with (
A.47
), (
A.67
) and (
A.68
) from (
A
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\mu_{k}-\beta\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}),\theta_{k}-\textnormal{mean}(\mu_{k},\theta_{k}))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C.

(A.69)

In light of (
A.17
), the latter estimates entail that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\;\mathrm{d}s+\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|(\mu_{k},\theta_{k})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C.

By standard compactness arguments, these estimates guarantee the existence of a limit quadruple
(\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})
solving (
A.1
) and satisfying the desired regularity properties. By Theorem
A.2
,
(\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})
is thus the unique strong solution to (
A.1
), which finishes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
On the bulk-surface Stokes equation

We consider a bulk-surface Stokes system with non-constant viscosity functions and a non-constant friction parameter on
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
d\in\{2,3\}
. The system reads as

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v})+\nabla p

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.1a)

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.1b)

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})+2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}\,\mathbf{n}]_{\tau}+\nabla_{\Gamma}q+\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma,

(B.1c)

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\,\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma,

(B.1d)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}|_{\Gamma}=\mathbf{w},\ \mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{n}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma.

(B.1e)

Here,
\phi:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\psi:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
are given measurable functions with
|\phi|\leq 1
a.e. in
\Omega
and
|\psi|\leq 1
a.e. on
\Gamma
, and the coefficients fulfill the following assumption:

(A)

It holds
\nu_{\Omega},\nu_{\Gamma}\in C([-1,1]
,
\gamma\in C([-1,1]^{2})
, and there exist positive constants
\nu_{\ast},\nu^{\ast},\gamma_{\ast},\gamma^{\ast}
such that

\displaystyle 0<\nu_{\ast}\leq\nu_{\Omega}(s),\nu_{\Gamma}(s)\leq\nu^{\ast}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}s\in[-1,1]

as well as

\displaystyle 0<\gamma_{\ast}\leq\gamma(s,r)\leq\gamma^{\ast}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}(s,r)\in[-1,1]^{2}.

Under this assumption, the authors of
[
30
]
proved the following theorem about the well-posedness of weak solutions to (
B.1
) (see
[
30
, Theorem 5.4]
):

Theorem B.1
.

Let
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\in(\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0})^{\prime}
. Then there exists a unique weak solution
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},p,q)
consisting of a pair of velocity fields
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}
and an associated pressure pair
(p,q)\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(0)}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}:\mathbf{D}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}p\,\textnormal{div}\,\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}q\,\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\,\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\big\langle(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}),(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\big\rangle_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}

for all
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}
. Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the systems, such that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}+\|(p,q)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\leq C\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{(\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0})^{\prime}}.

(B.2)

Additionally, in the case of constant viscosity functions and a constant friction function, the authors of
[
30
]
proved the existence of a strong solution and investigated the corresponding bulk-surface Stokes operator. The goal of this section is to generalize their results and show that the existence of strong solutions also holds for a more general class of coefficients. Namely, we assume the following condition:

(A)
′

It holds
\nu_{\Omega},\nu_{\Gamma}\in C^{2}([-1,1]
,
\gamma\in C([-1,1]^{2})
, and there exist positive constants
\nu_{\ast},\nu^{\ast},\gamma_{\ast},\gamma^{\ast}
such that

\displaystyle 0<\nu_{\ast}\leq\nu_{\Omega}(s),\nu_{\Gamma}(s)\leq\nu^{\ast}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}s\in[-1,1]

(B.3)

as well as

\displaystyle 0<\gamma_{\ast}\leq\gamma(s,r)\leq\gamma^{\ast}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}(s,r)\in[-1,1]^{2}.

Under these assumptions, the goal of this section is to prove the following theorem:

Theorem B.2
.

Let
(A)′
hold, and let
(\phi,\psi)\in\mathcal{W}^{1,\infty}
and
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\tau}(\Gamma)
. Then, there exists a unique solution
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},p,q)\in\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}\times\big(\mathcal{H}^{1}\cap\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(0)}\big)
to (
B.1
). Furthermore, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{2}}+\|(p,q)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\leq C\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}.

(B.4)

Similar to the proof of
[
30
, Theorem 5.9]
, the proof of Theorem
B.2
is based on Schauder’s fixed point theorem, by combining regularity theory for both the bulk and the surface Stokes equations. Therefore, we start by studying both equations separately.

B.1.
Non-homogeneous Stokes equation with non-constant viscosity

We consider

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v})+\nabla p

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.5a)

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.5b)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}|_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{w}_{\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma,

(B.5c)

for a given tangential vector field
\mathbf{w}_{\ast}:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We have the following result regarding the well-posedness of strong solutions to (
B.5
).

Lemma B.3
.

Let
\nu_{\Omega}\in C^{2}([-1,1])
satisfy (
B.3
),
\phi\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)
,
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
and
\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\in\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\tau}(\Gamma)
. Then, there exists a unique solution
(\mathbf{v},p)\in\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)\times(H^{1}(\Omega)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega))
to (
B.5
). Furthermore, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|p\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)}\big).

(B.6)

Proof.

Uniqueness.
According to the Lemma of Lax–Milgram, the corresponding homogeneous problem has a unique weak solution. As the zero solution trivially solves the homogeneous problem, the uniqueness of (
B.5
) readily follows.

Existence.
We first consider the system

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v})+\nabla p

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}|_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{w}_{\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma.

The existence of a unique solution
(\mathbf{v}_{1},p_{1})\in\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)\times(H^{1}(\Omega)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega))
has been proven in
[
8
]
for
d=3
, and in
[
40
]
for
d=2
, along with the existence of a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}_{1}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|p_{1}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C_{1}\big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)}\big).

Now, we consider

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v})+\nabla p

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}+\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.7a)

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.7b)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}|_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma.

(B.7c)

As the right-hand side of (
B.7a
) lies in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
by assumption on
\nu_{\Omega}
and the regularity of
\mathbf{v}_{1}
, using the Lemma of Lax–Milgram, one can show the existence of a unique
\mathbf{v}_{2}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{2}:\mathbf{D}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\big(\mathbf{f}+\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{1})\big)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x

for all
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}(\Omega)
. Hence, according to
[
1
, Section 4, Lemma 4]
, we additionally find that
\mathbf{v}_{2}\in\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)
. Then, applying integration by parts, for all
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}(\Omega)
it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big(-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{2})-\mathbf{f}-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{1})\Big)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x=0.

Consequently,
[
38
, Lemma II.2.2.2]
yields the existence of
p_{2}\in L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\nabla p_{2}=-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{2})-\mathbf{f}-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{1})

(B.8)

in the sense of distributions. As the right-hand side of (
B.8
) is an element of
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, we conclude
\nabla p_{2}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, and thus,
(\mathbf{v}_{2},p_{2})
is a strong solution to (
B.7
). Moreover, we have the following estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}_{2}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|p_{2}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C_{2}\big(\|\mathbf{v}_{1}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\big)

for a constant
C_{2}>0
. Finally, defining
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{2}
and
p=p_{2}
, we infer that
(\mathbf{v},p)
is a solution to (
B.5
) with the desired regularities and satisfies the estimate (
B.6
).
∎

B.2.
Surface Stokes equations with non-constant viscosity and friction

We consider

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})+\nabla_{\Gamma}q+\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma,

(B.9a)

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\,\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma.

(B.9b)

We prove the following result concerning the well-posedness of strong solutions to (
B.9
).

Lemma B.4
.

Let
\nu_{\Gamma}\in C^{2}([-1,1])
and
\gamma\in C([-1,1]^{2})
satisfy (
B.3
),
\psi\in W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma)
and
\mathbf{g}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\tau}(\Gamma)
. Then there exists a unique solution
(\mathbf{w},q)\in\mathbf{H}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)\times(H^{1}(\Gamma)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Gamma))
to (
B.9
). Additionally, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|q\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.

(B.10)

Proof.

We start by recalling the following surface Korn inequality,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq C_{\Gamma}\big(\|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Gamma)

for some constant
C_{\Gamma}>0
, see
[
24
, Eqn. (4.7)]
. Consequently, as both
\nu_{\Gamma}
and
\gamma
are bounded from below by a positive constant, the Lemma of Lax–Milgram guarantees the existence of a unique
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(B.11)

for all
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
along with a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.

(B.12)

Next, we observe that (
B.11
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{G}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(B.13)

for all
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
, where
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{g}+(1-\gamma(\phi,\psi))\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)
. In view of (
B.12
) and (
B.3
), it holds
\|\mathbf{G}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}
. Consequently, utilizing regularity theory for (
B.13
) (see
[
3
, Lemma 7.4]
), we deduce
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)
with

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{G}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

for some constant
C>0
. Finally, as
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)
, an application of integration by parts yields that

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\big(-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})+\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{g}\big)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=0

for all
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
. Thus, by definition of the projection
\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}
, we find
q\in\dot{H}^{1}(\Gamma)
such that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\Gamma}q=-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})+\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{g}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma.

(B.14)

Moreover, the choice of
q
is unique if we additionally require that
{\langle q\rangle}_{\Gamma}=0
. Then, by the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality, we obtain
q\in H^{1}(\Gamma)
. In this way,
(\mathbf{w},q)
is the unique solution to (
B.9
) satisfying the desired regularities as well as the estimate (
B.10
).
∎

B.3.
Bulk-surface Stokes equation

Now, having Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
B.4
at hand, we can finally prove Theorem
B.2
.

Proof of Theorem
B.2
.

Let
