\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
, and consider the unique pair
(\mathbf{v},p)\in\mathbf{H}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Omega)\times(H^{1}(\Omega)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega))
satisfying

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v})+\nabla p

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.15)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}|_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma,

which exists according to Lemma
B.3
. Additionally, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|p\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)}\big).

(B.16)

Then, since by the trace theorem it holds that
\mathbf{g}-2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}\,\mathbf{n}]_{\tau}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\tau}(\Gamma)
, Lemma
B.4
implies the existence of a unique pair
(\mathbf{w},q)\in\mathbf{H}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)\times(H^{1}(\Gamma)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Gamma))
solving

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})+\nabla_{\Gamma}q+\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}-2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}\,\mathbf{n}]_{\tau}

\displaystyle\text{on~}\Gamma,

(B.17)

\displaystyle\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\,\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\text{on~}\Gamma.

This defines a function
\mathbf{T}:\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)\rightarrow\mathbf{H}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
via
\mathbf{T}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\big)=\mathbf{w}
, with
\mathbf{w}
being the unique solution to (
B.17
). Now, let
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\in\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
, and consider the corresponding solutions
\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{2}
to (
B.17
) with
\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}
being the associated solutions to (
B.3
). Then, in view of the estimates (
B.10
) and (
B.16
), we find that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{T}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{1})-\mathbf{T}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{2})\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{1}\,\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{2}\,\mathbf{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}_{1}-\mathbf{v}_{2}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{1}-\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{2}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)}.

In particular, this shows that
\mathbf{T}
is continuous. As we have the compact embedding
\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)
, Schauder’s fixed-point theorem guarantees the existence of a fixed-point
\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\in\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)
such that
\mathbf{T}\big(\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\big)=\mathbf{w}_{\ast}
. Then, consider the corresponding solution
\mathbf{v}_{\ast}
to (
B.3
), and the pair
(p_{\ast},q_{\ast})
consisting of the bulk and surface pressure, respectively. In view of Lemma
B.1
,
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast},p_{\ast},q_{\ast})
is unique.
Lastly, to establish (
B.4
), we first note that due to (
2.2
), (
B.2
) and (
B.10
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|q_{\ast}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}&\leq C\big(\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\,\mathbf{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\big)\\
&\leq C\big(\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\\
&\leq\varepsilon\|\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+C_{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\end{split}

(B.18)

for some
\varepsilon>0
and a constant
C_{\varepsilon}>0
. Therefore, plugging this estimate into (
B.16
), and choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, we deduce

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|p_{\ast}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)}\big)\leq C\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}.

(B.19)

Finally, combining (
B.18
) and (
B.19
) yields (
B.4
), which finishes the proof.
∎

B.4.
Bulk-surface Stokes operator

We now consider the case of constant, non-negative coefficients. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\nu_{\Omega}=\nu_{\Gamma}\equiv 1
as well as
\gamma\equiv 1
. Then, the bulk-surface operator is defined as

\displaystyle\mathbfcal{A}:D(\mathbfcal{A})\subset\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}},

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(-\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}(\textnormal{div}(2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v})),-\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}(\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})+2[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}\,\mathbf{n}]_{\tau}+\mathbf{w})\big),

where

\displaystyle D(\mathbfcal{A})\coloneqq\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}.

It was shown in
[
29
]
that
\mathbfcal{A}
is a positive, unbounded, and self-adjoint operator on
\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}
with compact inverse.
Then, thanks to the above regularity results, we deduce that the operator
\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}:\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}\rightarrow\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}
is such that, for any
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\in\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}
, there exists
\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})=\big(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}),\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\big)\in D(\mathbfcal{A})
and
(p,q)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}\cap\mathcal{L}^{2}_{(0)}
which solve

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}))+\nabla p

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in~}\Omega,

(B.20a)

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}))+2[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\,\mathbf{n}]_{\tau}+\nabla_{\Gamma}q+\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on~}\Gamma.

(B.20b)

Furthermore, in view of (
B.4
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{2}}+\|(p,q)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\leq C\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}.

In particular, it holds that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{2}}\leq C\|\mathbfcal{A}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for all~}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}.

(B.21)

Lastly, we provide an
\mathcal{L}^{2}
estimate of the pressure pair
(p,q)
in (
B.20
), which can be seen as a generalization of
[
21
, Lemma B.2]
.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}
, such that

\displaystyle\|(p,q)\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}\leq C\|\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(B.22)

Proof.

Consider
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}
. Then, using the definition of the Stokes operator
\mathbfcal{A}
and integration by parts, we find that

\displaystyle\big((\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}),(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\big)_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\big(\mathbfcal{A}\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}),(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\big)_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=-\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}\big(\textnormal{div}(2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}))\big)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\big(\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}))\big)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\big(2[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\,\mathbf{n}]_{\tau}\big)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=-\int_{\Omega}\textnormal{div}(2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}))\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(2\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}))\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\mathbf{n}\,\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}):\mathbf{D}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}):\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\mathbf{n}\,\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\mathbf{n}\,\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

Recalling that
\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}:\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)\cap\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Omega)
as well as
\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}:\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Gamma)\rightarrow\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Gamma)\cap\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}(\Gamma)
are both bounded operators, respectively, we infer

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Omega}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}):\mathbf{D}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}):\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\big(\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{A}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\big)\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\|\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}.

Next, invoking Lemma
2.2
together with the estimate (
B.4
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|-\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\mathbf{n}\,\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\mathbf{n}\,\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}_{\Omega}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\|\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}.

Consequently, taking the supremum over all
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}
such that
\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}}\leq 1
, we find that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{(\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0})^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}+C\|\mathbfcal{A}^{-1}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

from which the desired inequality (
B.22
) immediately follows.
∎
